{"@context":"https:\/\/schema.org\/","@type":"BreadcrumbList","@id":"https:\/\/cyble.com\/blog\/fake-atomic-wallet-website-distributing-mars-stealer\/#breadcrumb","itemListElement":[{"@type":"ListItem","position":1,"item":{"@id":"https:\/\/cyble.com","name":"Cyble"}},{"@type":"ListItem","position":2,"item":{"@id":"https:\/\/cyble.com\/malware\/","name":"Malware"}},{"@type":"ListItem","position":3,"item":{"@id":"https:\/\/cyble.com\/cybercrime\/","name":"Cybercrime"}},{"@type":"ListItem","position":4,"item":{"@id":"https:\/\/cyble.com\/fake-app\/","name":"Fake App"}},{"@type":"ListItem","position":5,"item":{"@id":"https:\/\/cyble.com\/malware\/infostealer\/","name":"Infostealer"}},{"@type":"ListItem","position":6,"item":{"@id":"https:\/\/cyble.com\/stealer\/","name":"Stealer"}},{"@type":"ListItem","position":7,"item":{"@id":"https:\/\/cyble.com\/blog\/fake-atomic-wallet-website-distributing-mars-stealer\/","name":"Cyble - Fake Atomic Wallet Website Distributing Mars Stealer"}}]},

{"@context":"https:\/\/schema.org\/","@type":"NewsArticle","@id":"https:\/\/cyble.com\/blog\/fake-atomic-wallet-website-distributing-mars-stealer\/#newsarticle","url":"https:\/\/cyble.com\/blog\/fake-atomic-wallet-website-distributing-mars-stealer\/","headline":"Cyble - Fake Atomic Wallet Website Distributing Mars Stealer","mainEntityOfPage":"https:\/\/cyble.com\/blog\/fake-atomic-wallet-website-distributing-mars-stealer\/","datePublished":"2022-08-02T10:35:03-04:00","dateModified":"2022-08-02T10:35:46-04:00","description":"Cyble analyzes a fake Atomic Wallet website that is being used to distribute Mars Stealer to cryptocurrency users.","articleSection":"Stealer","articleBody":"Info Stealer Targeting Browsers and Crypto Wallets        The popularity of Cryptocurrency has increased exponentially over the recent years as dealing with crypto has become relatively hassle-free and more accessible. The financial returns of crypto investments have attracted many investors to invest in crypto markets.    As the demand for crypto investment has increased over the years, we can also see a corresponding rise in the number of crypto wallets. Some popular crypto wallets such as Binance, Atomic, Exodus, Coinbase, Metamask, and Trust are the most commonly used platforms to manage and transact Cryptocurrency.    Despite gaining popularity worldwide, Cryptocurrency also has its downsides. It opens the door for various malicious activities like phishing, scams, hacking, delivering malware, etc.    Cyble Research Labs has constantly been tracking malicious activities targeting Cryptocurrency wallets. During a routine threat-hunting exercise, we came across a Twitter post where a researcher mentioned a fake Atomic wallet site distributing Mars Stealer.    The phishing site \"hxxp:\/\/atomic-walletnet\" uses the icon and name of the Atomic wallet. Additionally, the Threat Actor is trying to copy the UI of a genuine website to trick the user, as shown in the below image.    Figure 1 \u2013 Phishing site impersonating Atomic Wallet website        Upon investigating the phishing site, we observed that the TA has invested time in developing a well-designed phishing site to trick victims into downloading the malware.    The phishing site appears to be genuine as the TA provided some attractive content such as Trusted Reviews, Cashback, FAQ, Partners, Contact Us page, Support, and Update History.    Figure 2 \u2013 Content on Phishing site to appear legitimate        When the user interacts with the \"Download\" button, the phishing site redirects to the download options page, where the user can download Atomic wallet for Windows, iOS, and Android, as shown in the below image.    Figure 3 \u2013 Download options for the user        The App Store button is inactive while, the Google Play button redirects the user to the genuine Atomic Wallet Play Store link.    When the user clicks on the \"Download for Windows\" button, it connects to shortened URL \"hxxps:\/\/bitly\/3PRDyH8\" and downloads a Zip file named \"Atomic Wallet.zip\".    After a detailed investigation, the downloaded file was identified as a Mars Stealer sample. Mars Stealer was discovered in June 2021 and was available for sale on a few underground cybercrime forums. Mars stealer primarily targets browser extensions, crypto extensions and wallets, and 2FA plugins.    Technical Analysis        The downloaded Zip file contains the \"AtomicWallet-Setup.bat\" file containing malicious code, as shown in the below image.    Figure 4 \u2013 Downloaded Zip file content        Upon execution, the .bat file invokes the Powershell command, enabling the administrative elevation for its execution.    Figure 5 \u2013 Executing PowerShell command for admin privileges \u00a0        The .bat file then copies powershell.exe into the current directory, renames it as AtomicWallet_Setup.bat.exe, and then hides it using the attrib command.        Figure 6 \u2013 Hiding the .exe file using the attrib command        Then, the .bat file executes PowerShell content using AtomicWallet_Setup.bat.exe, which further decodes the base64-encoded content and decrypts it using an AES algorithm that stores a Gzip Compressed stream in the memory.    The below figure shows the code used by the malware to perform AES decryption and GZip Decompression.    Figure 7 \u2013 Code for AES Decryption and GZip Decompression        Finally, the malware decompresses the GZip content and loads the final PowerShell code that downloads Mars Stealer from the Discord server to the victim's %LOCALAPPDATA% location.        Figure 8 \u2013 Downloading Mars Stealer from the Discord server        The below figure shows the infection chain of Mars Stealer. After downloading Mars stealer, the .bat file deletes the \"AtomicWallet_Setup.bat.exe\" from the victim's machine.    Figure 9 \u2013 Infection chain        After successful installation, Mars Stealer steals sensitive information from the victim's device and exfiltrates the stolen data to the C&amp;C server.    Figure 10 \u2013 Malware sending stolen data to the C&amp;C server        Conclusion        According to our research, the TAs behind Mars stealer are adopting sophisticated phishing attacks to distribute Mars Stealer and gather user credentials, system information, and other sensitive data.    The criminals may use compromised credentials to carry out attacks to stay under the radar and avoid tripping any security monitoring rules, thus alerting any victims to the attempted compromise.    Our Recommendations        Avoid downloading pirated software from unverified sites.Use strong passwords and enforce multi-factor authentication wherever possible.\u202fKeep updating your passwords after certain intervals.Use a reputed anti-virus and internet security software package on your connected devices, including PC, laptop, and mobile.\u202f\u00a0Refrain from opening untrusted links and email attachments without first verifying their authenticity.\u202f\u202f\u00a0Block URLs that could be used to spread the malware, e.g., Torrent\/Warez.\u202f\u00a0Monitor the beacon on the network level to block data exfiltration by malware or TAs.\u202f\u00a0Enable Data Loss Prevention (DLP) Solutions on employees' systems.\u202f    MITRE ATT&amp;CK\u00ae Techniques        TacticTechnique IDTechnique NameInitial AccessT1566PhishingExecutionT1204User ExecutionDefense EvasionT1564Hidden Files and DirectoryDefense EvasionT1027Obfuscated Files or InformationCredential AccessT1555 T1539T1552 T1528Credentials from Password Stores Steal Web Session Cookies Unsecured Credentials Steal Application Access TokenDiscoveryT1082System Information data-widget_type="theme-post-title.default"> <div class="elementor-widget-container"> <h1 class="elementor-heading-title elementor-size-default">Fake Atomic Wallet Website Distributing Mars Stealer</h1> </div> </div> <div class="elementor-element elementor-element-1fcc1d6c elementor-widget elementor-widget-theme-post-excerpt" data-id="1fcc1d6c" data-element_type="widget" data-widget_type="theme-post-excerpt.default"> <div class="elementor-widget-container"> Cyble analyzes a fake Atomic Wallet website that is being used to distribute Mars Stealer to cryptocurrency users. </div> </div> <div class="elementor-element elementor-element-2907e1e2 blog_content elementor-widget elementor-widget-theme-post-content" data-id="2907e1e2" data-element_type="widget" data-widget_type="theme-post-content.default"> <div class="elementor-widget-container"> <h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="h-info-stealer-targeting-browsers-and-crypto-wallets">Info Stealer Targeting Browsers and Crypto Wallets</h2> <p></p> <p>The popularity of Cryptocurrency has increased exponentially over the recent years as dealing with crypto has become relatively hassle-free and more accessible. The financial returns of crypto investments have attracted many investors to invest in crypto markets.</p> <p>As the demand for crypto investment has increased over the years, we can also see a corresponding rise in the number of crypto wallets. Some popular crypto wallets such as Binance, Atomic, Exodus, Coinbase, Metamask, and Trust are the most commonly used platforms to manage and transact Cryptocurrency.</p> <div id="om-wlravxmwms40sunr2q0v-holder"></div> <template data-nitro-marker-id="a63c15a9ea9c95cbcf4be387544881a9-1"></template>  <p>Despite gaining popularity worldwide, Cryptocurrency also has its downsides. It opens the door for various malicious activities like phishing, scams, hacking, delivering <a class="wpil_keyword_link" href="https://cyble.com/knowledge-hub/what-is-malware/" title="malware" data-wpil-keyword-link="linked" data-wpil-monitor-id="430" target="_blank">malware</a>, etc.</p> <p>Cyble Research Labs has constantly been tracking malicious activities targeting Cryptocurrency wallets. During a routine threat-hunting exercise, we came across a <a href="https://twitter.com/ViriBack/status/1554137490872799233?t=T8KXijmWIb3omyiPlJyQYg&amp;s=19" target="_blank" rel="nofollow noopener">Twitter post</a> where a researcher mentioned a fake Atomic wallet site distributing Mars Stealer.</p> <p>The phishing site &#8220;hxxp://atomic-wallet[.]net&#8221; uses the icon and name of the Atomic wallet. Additionally, the Threat Actor is trying to copy the UI of a genuine website to trick the user, as shown in the below image.</p> <figure class="wp-block-image aligncenter size-large"><img width="1024" height="528" data-attachment-id="12173" data-permalink="https://cyble.com/blog/fake-atomic-wallet-website-distributing-mars-stealer/figure-1-phishing-site-impersonating-atomic-wallet-website/" invested time in developing a well-designed phishing site to trick victims into downloading the malware.</p><div class="cyble-content_6" id="cyble-930468200"><div the video tag. </video> </div> </div> <p>The phishing site appears to be genuine as the TA provided some attractive content such as Trusted Reviews, Cashback, FAQ, Partners, Contact Us page, Support, class="wp-element-caption"><em>Figure 3 – Download options for the user</em></figcaption></figure> <p></p> <p>The App Store button is inactive while, the Google Play button redirects the user to the genuine Atomic Wallet Play Store link.</p> <p>When the user clicks on the &#8220;<em>Download for Windows</em>&#8221; button, it connects to shortened URL &#8220;<em>hxxps://bit[.]ly/3PRDyH8</em>&#8221; and downloads a Zip file named &#8220;<em>Atomic Wallet.zip</em>&#8220;.</p> <p>After a detailed investigation, the downloaded file was identified as a Mars Stealer sample. Mars Stealer was discovered in June 2021 and was available for sale on a few underground cybercrime forums. Mars stealer primarily targets browser extensions, crypto extensions and wallets, and 2FA plugins.</p> <h2 class="wp-block-heading">Technical Analysis</h2> <p></p> <p>The downloaded Zip file contains the &#8220;AtomicWallet-Setup.bat&#8221; file containing malicious code, as shown in the below image.</p> <figure class="wp-block-image aligncenter size-full"><img width="777" height="208" the current directory<em>, </em>renames it as <em>AtomicWallet_Setup.bat.exe,</em> and then hides it using the <em>attrib</em> command.</p> <p></p> <figure class="wp-block-image aligncenter class="wp-element-caption"><em>Figure 10 – Malware sending stolen data to the C&amp;C server</em></figcaption></figure> <p></p> <h2 class="wp-block-heading">Conclusion</h2> <p></p> <p>According to our research, the TAs behind Mars stealer are adopting sophisticated phishing attacks to distribute Mars Stealer and gather user credentials, system information, and other sensitive data.</p> <p>The criminals may use compromised credentials to carry out attacks to stay under the radar and avoid tripping any security monitoring rules, thus alerting any victims to the attempted compromise.</p> <h2 class="wp-block-heading">Our Recommendations</h2> <p></p> <ul class="wp-block-list"><li>Avoid downloading pirated software from unverified sites.</li><li>Use strong passwords and enforce multi-factor authentication wherever possible. </li><li>Keep updating your passwords after certain intervals.</li><li>Use a reputed anti-virus and internet security software package on your connected devices, including PC, laptop, and mobile. &nbsp;</li><li>Refrain from opening untrusted links and email attachments without first verifying their authenticity. &nbsp;</li><li>Block URLs that could be used to spread the malware, e.g., Torrent/Warez. &nbsp;</li><li>Monitor the beacon on the network level to block data exfiltration by malware or TAs. &nbsp;</li><li>Enable Data Loss Prevention (DLP) Solutions on employees&#8217; systems. </li></ul> <h2 class="wp-block-heading">MITRE ATT&amp;CK® Techniques</h2> <p></p> <figure
